B.3.
Mode Theory

In the terminology of MTT
(
Gratzer et al. 2020
)
, effect contexts are

modes
.
The structure of modes, modalities, and modality transformation
constitute the
mode theory
.

To make the proofs easier, we frequently write modalities in the form

\mu_{F}
as introduced in
Section

3.3
.
Supposing
\mu(F)=E
, we read
\mu_{F}
as a morphism
E\to F
from
mode
E
to mode
F
.
The reading of
\mu_{F}
as a morphism between modes is consistent with
definition of modalities in MTT, while
\mu
itself is actually an
indexed family of morphisms.
We call
\mu_{F}

concrete modalities
since we have already
called
\mu
modalities.
We repeat the definitions of modalities and modality composition using
syntax
\mu_{F}
for easy reference. They are the same as those in

Section

3.3
.

\begin{array}[]{rcr@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}[E]_{F}&:&E&\to&F\\
{\langle D\rangle}_{F}&:&D+F&\to&F\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rclcll}[E^{\prime}]_{F}&\circ&[E]_{E^{\prime}}&=&[E]_{F}\\
{\langle D\rangle}_{F}&\circ&[E]_{D+F}&=&[E]_{F}\\
[E]_{F}&\circ&{\langle D\rangle}_{E}&=&[D+E]_{F}\\
{\langle D_{1}\rangle}_{F}&\circ&{\langle D_{2}\rangle}_{D_{1}+F}&=&{\langle D_{2}+D_{1}\rangle}_{F}\\
\end{array}

We write
D+E
for
D,E
and
D+D^{\prime}
for
D,D^{\prime}
for notation
consistency with
Met
.
We also write
\Gamma\vdash\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}
for

\Gamma\vdash\mu\Rightarrow\nu\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}
.
As we extend composition to concrete modalities, we also let the
operation
\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma)
return a concrete modality as follows.

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\mathsf{locks}(\cdot)={{\langle\rangle}_{F}}\qquad\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}})=\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma)\circ{\mu_{F}}\qquad\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A)=\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma)\\
\end{array}

Appendix C
Meta Theory and Proofs for
Met
(
\mathcal{X}
)

We provide meta theory and proofs for
Met
(
\mathcal{X}
) introduced in

Section

3
.
The proofs are based on the proofs for
Met
in
Tang et al. 2025
but
are parameterised over the effect theory
\mathcal{X}
.
We require the effect theory to satisfy the validity conditions in

Definition

3.5
.
2
2

2

Our proofs (especially the
proof of progress in
Section

C.3
) only use the second validity
condition. The first condition is not necessary to show progress and
subject reduction but it is natural. We opt for keeping the first
condition.

C.1.
Properties of the Mode Theory of
Met
(
\mathcal{X}
)

Our proofs for type soundness rely on some properties of the mode
theory of
Met
(
\mathcal{X}
).

First, the mode theory of
Met
(
\mathcal{X}
) should form a double category.
The effect contexts and subeffecting (a preorder relation) obviously
form a category generated by a poset.
The effect contexts (objects) and modalities (horizontal morphisms)
also form a category since modality composition possesses
associativity and identity.
We have the following lemma.

Lemma C.1 (Modes and modalities form a category).

Modes and modalities form a category with the identity morphisms

\mathbbm{1}_{E}={\langle\rangle}{}_{E}:E\to E
and the morphism composition

\mu_{F}\circ\nu_{F^{\prime}}
such that

(1)

Identity:
\mathbbm{1}_{F}\circ\mu_{F}=\mu_{F}=\mu_{F}\circ\mathbbm{1}_{E}
for
\mu_{F}:E\to F
.

(2)

Associativity:
(\mu_{E_{1}}\circ\nu_{E_{2}})\circ\xi_{E_{3}}=\mu_{E_{1}}\circ(\nu_{E_{2}}\circ\xi_{E_{3}})

for
\mu_{E_{1}}:E_{2}\to E_{1}
,
\nu_{E_{2}}:E_{3}\to E_{2}
, and
\xi_{E_{3}}:E\to E_{3}
.

Proof.

By inlining the definitions of modalities and checking each case.
∎

As in
Tang et al. 2025
, we need to extend the modality
transformation relation a bit for meta theory and proofs.
We write
\Gamma\vdash\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}
if

\Gamma\vdash\mu\Rightarrow\nu\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}
.
We extend it to allow judgements of form

\Gamma\vdash\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F^{\prime}}
where
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
and add one new
rule
MT-Mono
as follows.

{\frac{\Gamma\vdash F\leqslant F^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu_{F^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{MT-Mono}}}}

Now we show that modality transformations are 2-cells in the double category.

Lemma C.2 (Modality transformations are 2-cells).

If
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F^{\prime}}
,
\mu_{F}:E\to F
, and
\nu_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
,
then
E\leqslant E^{\prime}
and
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
.
Moreover, the transformation relation is closed under vertical and
horizontal composition as shown by the following admissible rules.

\frac{\mu_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\nu_{F_{2}}\quad\nu_{F_{2}}\Rightarrow\xi_{F_{3}}}{\mu_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\xi_{F_{3}}}\par\frac{\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}\quad\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}\quad\mu_{F}:E\to F\quad\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}\quad}{\mu_{F}\circ\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}\circ\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}}

Proof.

For the first part,
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
is obvious from
MT-Mono
.

E\leqslant E^{\prime}
follows from case analysis.

Case

\mu=[E]
. Obvious from
MT-Abs
and
Lemma

C.3
.

Case

\mu={\langle D_{1}\rangle}
and
\nu={\langle D_{2}\rangle}
. We need to
show that
D_{1},F\leqslant D_{2},F^{\prime}
.
By
MT-Extend
we have
D_{1},F^{\prime}\leqslant D_{2},F^{\prime}
, which gives

D_{1},F^{\prime},F_{1}\equiv D_{2},F^{\prime}
for some
F_{1}
.
By
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
we have
F,F_{2}\equiv F^{\prime}
for some
F_{2}
. Then
we have
D_{1},F,F_{2},F_{1}\equiv D_{2},F^{\prime}
.
Finally we have
D_{1},F\leqslant D_{2},F^{\prime}
.

For the second part, vertical composition (the first rule) basically
says that modality transformation is transitive. Easy to verify.
Horizontal composition (the second rule) follows from a
straightforward case analysis on shapes of modalities being
composed.

Case

\nu_{E}
is an absolute modality. Suppose
\mu=[E_{1}]
. We have
(\mu\circ\nu)(F_{1})=E_{1}
for any
F^{\prime}\leqslant F_{1}
. By
Lemma

C.3
, we have
E_{1}\leqslant(\mu^{\prime}\circ\nu^{\prime})(F_{1})
.

Case

\nu_{E}
is an relative modality and
\mu_{F}
is an
absolute modality.
Suppose
\mu=[E_{1}]
and
\nu={\langle D_{1}\rangle}
. We have

(\mu\circ\nu)(F_{1})=D_{1}+E_{1}
for any
F^{\prime}\leqslant F_{1}
. Similar
to the above case, by
Lemma

C.3
, we have
D_{1}+E_{1}\leqslant(\mu^{\prime}\circ\nu^{\prime})(F_{1})
.

Case

Both
\mu_{F}
and
\nu_{E}
are relative modalities. We
also have that
\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}
and
\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}
are relative
modalities.
Suppose
\mu={\langle D_{1}\rangle},\nu={\langle D_{2}\rangle},\mu^{\prime}={\langle D_{1}^{\prime}\rangle},\nu^{\prime}={\langle D_{2}^{\prime}\rangle}
.
We have
E=D_{1},F
and
E^{\prime}=D_{1}^{\prime},F^{\prime}
.
By
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}
and
MT-Extend
, we have

D_{1},F_{1}\leqslant D_{1}^{\prime},F_{1}

for all
F^{\prime}\leqslant F_{1}
.
There exists
F_{1}^{\prime}
such that

{\color[rgb]{1,0.2,0.2}{D_{1},F_{1},F_{1}^{\prime}\equiv D_{1}^{\prime},F_{1}}\,(1)}

By
\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}
and
MT-Extend
, we have

D_{2},F_{2}\leqslant D_{2}^{\prime},F_{2}

for all
E^{\prime}=D_{1}^{\prime},F^{\prime}\leqslant F_{2}
.
There exists
F_{2}^{\prime}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,1}{D_{2},F_{2},F_{2}^{\prime}\equiv D_{2}^{\prime},F_{2}}\,(2)}

Given any
F^{\prime}\leqslant F_{3}
, by
(1)
we can find
F_{31}
such that

D_{1},F_{3},F_{31}\equiv D_{1}^{\prime},F_{3}

Then by
D_{1}^{\prime},F^{\prime}\leqslant D_{1}^{\prime},F_{3}
and
(2)
we can find
F_{32}
such that

D_{2},D_{1},F_{3},F_{31},F_{32}\equiv D_{2}^{\prime},D_{1}^{\prime},F_{3}

Then we have

D_{2},D_{1},F_{3}\leqslant D_{2}^{\prime},D_{1}^{\prime},F_{3}

Finally by
MT-Extend
we have
{\langle D_{2},D_{1}\rangle}_{F}\Rightarrow{\langle D_{2}^{\prime},D_{1}^{\prime}\rangle}_{F^{\prime}}
.

∎

Beyond being a double category, we show some extra properties.
The most important one is that horizontal morphisms (sub-effecting)
act functorially on vertical ones (modalities). In other words, the
action of
\mu
on effect contexts gives a total monotone function.

Lemma C.3 (Monotone modalities).

If
\mu_{F}:E\to F
and
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
, then

\mu_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
with
E\leqslant E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

When
\mu
is an absolute modality, obviously we have
E\equiv E^{\prime}
.
When
\mu
is a relative modality
{\langle D\rangle}
, we need to show that

D+F\leqslant D+F^{\prime}
, which is obvious by
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma C.4 (Soundness of modality transformation).

For modality transformation
\Gamma\vdash\mu\Rightarrow\nu\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}
, we
have
\mu(F^{\prime})\leqslant\nu(F^{\prime})
for all
F^{\prime}
with

F\leqslant F^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By case analysis on the two modality transformation rules.

Case

MT-Abs
. Follow from
Lemma

C.3
.

Case

MT-Extend
. By definition.

∎

We state some properties of the mode theory as the following lemmas
for easier references in proofs. Most of them directly follow from the
definition.

Lemma C.5 (Vertical composition).

If
\mu_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\nu_{F_{2}}
and
\nu_{F_{2}}\Rightarrow\xi_{F_{3}}
, then
\mu_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\xi_{F_{3}}
.

Proof.

Follow from
Lemma

C.2

∎

Lemma C.6 (Horizontal composition).

If
\mu_{F}:E\to F
,
\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
,
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}
, and

\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}
, then
\mu_{F}\circ\nu_{E}\Rightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{F^{\prime}}\circ\nu^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Follow from
Lemma

C.2

∎

Lemma C.7 (Monotone modality transformation).

If
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}
and
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
,
then
\mu_{F^{\prime}}\Rightarrow\nu_{F^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By a case analysis.

Case

MT-Abs
. Follow from
Lemma

C.3
.

Case

MT-Extend
. By definition.

∎

Lemma C.8 (Asymmetric reflexivity of modality transformation).

If
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
and
\mu_{F}:E\to F
,
then
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\mu_{F^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By
MT-Mono
.
∎

C.2.
Lemmas for the Calculus

We prove structural and substitution lemmas for
Met
(
\mathcal{X}
) as well as some
other auxiliary lemmas for proving type soundness.

Lemma C.9 (Canonical forms).

1.

If
\,\vdash U:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is of shape
{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,U^{\prime}
.

2.

If
\,\vdash U:A\to B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is of shape
\lambda x^{A}.M
.

3.

If
\,\vdash U:\forall\alpha^{K}.A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is of shape
\Lambda\alpha^{K}.V
.

4.

If
\,\vdash U:{\texttt{1}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, then
U
is
{()}
.

Proof.

Directly follows from the typing rules.
∎

In order to define the lock weakening lemma, we first define a context
update operation
{\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}
which gives a new context
derived from updating the indexes of all locks and variable bindings
in
\Gamma
such that
\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):E\to F^{\prime}
for
some
E
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\llparenthesis\cdot\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\cdot\\
{\llparenthesis\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[E]_{F^{\prime}}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[E]_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\\
{\llparenthesis\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle D\rangle}_{F^{\prime}}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle D\rangle}_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{D+F}\\
{\llparenthesis x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F^{\prime}}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F^{\prime}}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}\\
{\llparenthesis\alpha:K,\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\alpha:K,{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}\\
{\llparenthesis\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B,\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}&=&\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B,{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F}\\
\end{array}

We have the following lemma showing that the index update operation
preserves the
\mathsf{locks}(-)
operation except for updating the index.

Lemma C.10 (Index update preserves composition).

If
\mu_{F}=\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma):E\to F
,
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
, and

\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
, then
\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}})=\mu_{F^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on the context and using the property
that
(\mu\circ\nu)_{F}=\mu_{F}\circ\nu_{E}
for
\mu_{F}:E\to F
.
∎

Corollary C.11 (Index update preserves transformation).

If
\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma):E\to F
,
F\leqslant F^{\prime}
, and

\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}
, then
\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma)\Rightarrow\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

Immediately follow from
Lemma

C.10
and

Lemma

C.8
.
∎

We have the following structural lemmas.

Lemma C.12 (Structural rules).

The following structural rules are admissible.

1.

Variable weakening.

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

2.

Variable swapping.

\frac{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,y\@ifmtarg{\nu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}}}B,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:A^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma,y\@ifmtarg{\nu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}}}B,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:A^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

3.

Lock weakening.

\frac{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}\quad\nu_{F}:F^{\prime}\to F\quad\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}):E^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{F^{\prime}}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

4.

Type variable weakening.

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma,\alpha:K,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

5.

Type variable swapping.

\frac{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\alpha:K,\Gamma_{3}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma_{1},\alpha:K,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{3}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\par\frac{\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma_{2})\quad{\Gamma_{1},\alpha:K,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{3}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}}{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\alpha:K,\Gamma_{3}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

6.

Label weakening.

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}{\Gamma,\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

7.

Lock swapping.

\frac{{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}},x\@ifmtarg{\nu}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\Gamma\vdash A:\mathsf{Abs}\text{ or }\nu=[F]\quad}{\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\nu}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu}}}}A,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

Proof.

1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7 follow from straightforward induction on the typing
derivation.
Among them, 7 makes use of the transformation rule
MT-Abs
.
3 follows from a induction very similar to that in

Tang et al. 2025
.
The most interesting cases are
T-Do
and
T-Handle
due to
the abstraction over the effect mode theory.
We show them as follows.

Case

\frac{\Sigma,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\ni\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0.2,0.2}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash N:A\;{{@}}\,{{\ell,E}}}\,(1)}\quad}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;N:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\ell,E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Do}}}

Suppose
\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime})=\xi_{\mu(F)}
.
We have
\ell\leqslant\xi(\mu(F))
.
By
Lemma

C.10
we have
\mathsf{locks}({\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{\nu(F)})=\xi_{\nu(F)}

By IH on
(1)
we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\nu_{F}}}},{\llparenthesis\Gamma^{\prime}\rrparenthesis}_{\nu(F)}\vdash N:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\xi(\mu(F))}}

By
Lemma

C.2
and
\mu_{F}\Rightarrow\nu_{F}
we have

\mu(F)\leqslant\nu(F)
.
By
Lemma

C.3
we have
\xi(\mu(F))\leqslant\xi(\nu(F))
.
By transitivity of subeffecting we have
\ell\leqslant\xi(\nu(F))
.
Finally our goal follows from reapplying
T-Do
.

Case

\frac{\mu(F)=E\quad\Gamma,\xi_{F_{1}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mu\Rightarrow{\langle\rangle}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}\quad\Gamma,\xi_{F_{1}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mu\Rightarrow\mu\circ\mu\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0.2,0.2}{\Gamma,\xi_{F_{1}},\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{{\langle\ell\rangle}_{E}}}}\vdash M:A\;{{@}}\,{{\ell,E}}}\,(1)}\quad\Sigma,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\ni\ell:A^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,1}{\Gamma,\xi_{F_{1}},\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},x:{{{(\mu\circ{\langle\ell\rangle})}}}A\vdash N:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(2)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\Gamma,\xi_{F_{1}},\Gamma^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},p:A^{\prime},r:{{{\mu}}}(B^{\prime}\to B)\vdash N^{\prime}:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(3)}\quad}{\Gamma,\xi_{F_{1}},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\mu}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto N,\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N^{\prime}\}:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handle}}}

Suppose we want to do the lock weakening
\xi_{F_{1}}\Rightarrow\nu_{F_{1}}
.
Our goal follows from IHs on
(1)
,
(2)
,
(3)
and
reapplying
T-Handle
.
As in the case of
T-Do
, we need to make sure that after IH on

(1)
the label
\ell
is still in the effect context.
This is guaranteed by the lock
\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle\ell\rangle}}}}
.

∎

The following lemma reflects the intuition that pure values can be
used in any effect context.

Lemma C.13 (Pure Promotion).

The following promotion rule is admissible.

\frac{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad\Gamma_{1}\vdash A:\mathsf{Abs}\quad\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}\quad\mathsf{fv}(V)\cap\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)=\emptyset\quad}{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}}

Proof.

Follow from the proof for the same lemma in
Tang et al. 2025
.
∎

Lemma C.14 (Generalised Pure Promotion).

Given
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
, if for any
x\in\mathsf{ftv}(M)
, we
have
\Gamma_{1}\ni x\@ifmtarg{\mu}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu}}}}B
such that either
\Gamma_{1}\vdash B:\mathsf{Abs}
or
\mu
is an absolute modality, then

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}
for
E\leqslant E^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E^{\prime}}}
.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on typing judgements in
Met
(
\mathcal{X}
).
The most non-trivial case is to show the accessibility of each
variable
x\in\mathsf{ftv}(M)
.
For variables with types of kind
\mathsf{Abs}
, we can always access them.
For variables annotated with an absolute modality, the modality
transformation relation
MT-Abs
still holds because
E\leqslant E^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma C.15 (Substitution).

The following substitution rules are admissible.

1.

Preservation of kinds under type substitution.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash A:K\quad\Gamma,\alpha:K,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash B:K}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash B[A/\alpha]:K}

2.

Preservation of types under type substitution.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash A:K\quad\Gamma,\alpha:K,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}\quad}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M[A/\alpha]:B[A/\alpha]\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}

3.

Preservation of types under value substitution.

\frac{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}\vdash V:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F^{\prime}}}\quad\Gamma,x\@ifmtarg{\mu_{F}}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}}A,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}\quad}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M[V/x]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}

Proof.

1,2,4,5. Follow from straightforward induction.

3. Follow from the proof for the same lemma in

Tang et al. 2025
.
The new cases of
T-LocalEffect
and generalised
T-Handle

simply follows from IH.
∎

C.3.
Progress

See
3.7

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation

\Omega\mid\cdot\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
. Most cases are covered in the
proof for progress of
Met
in
Tang et al. 2025
. We show some
important cases.

Case

T-Do
.
{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;M
. We have
\ell\leqslant E
. Either

M
is reducible or the whole term is in a normal form with respect to

E
.

Case

T-LocalEffect
. Reducible by
E-Gen
.

Case

T-Handle
.
\Omega\mid\cdot\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\mu}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto N,\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N^{\prime}\}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
.

Case

M
is reducible. Trivial.

Case

M
is a value. By
E-Ret
.

Case

M=\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell^{\prime}\;U]
.
Since
M
is not reducible itself, there is no handler for
\ell^{\prime}

in
\mathcal{E}
.
If
\ell=\ell^{\prime}
, by
E-Op
. Otherwise, since
M
is at effect
context
\ell,E
, by inversion on
{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell^{\prime}\;U
we have

\ell^{\prime}\leqslant D,\ell,E
where
D
does not contain label
\ell^{\prime}
.
By
Definition

3.5
we have
\ell^{\prime}\leqslant E
. Thus the
whole term is in a normal form with respect to
E
.

∎

C.4.
Subject Reduction

See
3.8

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation

\Omega\mid\Gamma\vdash M:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}
. Most cases are covered in the
proof for subject reduction of
Met
in
Tang et al. 2025
. We show
some important cases, among which the most non-trivial one is for our
modality-parameterised handlers.

Case

T-Do
. The only way to reduce is by
E-Lift
.
Follow from IH and reapplying
T-Do
.

Case

\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0.2,0.2}{\Omega\mid\Gamma,\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\vdash M:A^{\prime}\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(1)}\quad}{\Omega\mid\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{local}}}\;\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;M:A^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-LocalEffect}}}

The only way to reduce is by
E-Gen
.
By
(1)
we have

\Omega,\ell^{\prime}:A\twoheadrightarrow B\mid\Gamma\vdash M[\ell^{\prime}/\ell]:A^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}

Note that
\ell
cannot appear in
A^{\prime}
and
E
.

Case

{\frac{H=\{{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto N,\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N^{\prime}\}\quad\Sigma,\Gamma\ni\ell:A^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime}\quad\mu(F)=E\quad\Gamma\vdash\mu\Rightarrow{\langle\rangle}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}\quad\Gamma\vdash\mu\Rightarrow\mu\circ\mu\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0.2,0.2}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{{\langle\ell\rangle}_{E}}}}\vdash M:A\;{{@}}\,{{\ell,E}}}\,(1)}\quad\quad{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,1}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},x:{{{(\mu\circ{\langle\ell\rangle})}}}A\vdash N:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(2)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},p:A^{\prime},r:{{{\mu}}}(B^{\prime}\to B)\vdash N^{\prime}:B\;{{@}}\,{{E}}}\,(3)}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\mu}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handle}}}}

By case analysis on the reduction.

Case

E-Lift
with
M\mathrel{\leadsto}M^{\prime}
. By IHs and
reapplying
T-Handle
.

Case

E-Ret
. We have
M=U
and

{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;U\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\mathrel{\leadsto}N[({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu\circ{\langle\ell\rangle}}\;U)/x].

By
(1)
and
T-Mod
, we have

\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu\circ{\langle\ell\rangle}}\;U:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{(\mu\circ{\langle\ell\rangle})}}}A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}

By
(2)
,
\mu\Rightarrow{\langle\rangle}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}
, and
Lemma

C.12
.3, we have

{\Gamma,x:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{(\mu\circ{\langle\ell\rangle})}}}A\vdash N:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}}\\

Then by
Lemma

C.15
.3 we have

\Gamma\vdash N[({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu\circ{\langle\ell\rangle}}\;U)/x]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}

Case

E-Op
.
We have
M=\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;U]
and

{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\mu}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\mathrel{\leadsto}N^{\prime}[U/p,({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,(\lambda y.{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\mu}\;\mathcal{E}[y]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H))/r]

By
(3)
,
\mu\Rightarrow{\langle\rangle}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}
, and
Lemma

C.12
.3, we have

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:{{{\mu}}}(B^{\prime}\to B)\vdash N^{\prime}:B\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(4)}

By inversion on
{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;U
, we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle\ell\rangle}_{E}}}}\vdash U:A^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\ell,E}}

By
A^{\prime}:\mathsf{Abs}
and
Lemma

C.13
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{\Gamma\vdash U:A^{\prime}\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(5)}.

By
(1)
,
\mu\Rightarrow\mu\circ\mu\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}
, and

Lemma

C.12
.3, supposing
\mu(E)=E^{\prime}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{F}}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{\mu_{E}}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{{{\langle\ell\rangle}_{E^{\prime}}}}}\vdash M:A\;{{@}}\,{{\ell,E^{\prime}}}}\,(6)}

Observe that
B^{\prime}:\mathsf{Abs}
allows
y
to be accessed in any context.
By
(6)
and a straightforward induction on
\mathcal{E}
we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},y:B^{\prime},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{E}}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle\ell\rangle}_{E^{\prime}}}}}\vdash\mathcal{E}[y]:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\ell,E^{\prime}}}

Then by
T-Handle
we have

\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}},y:B^{\prime}\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\mu}\;\mathcal{E}[y]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H:A\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}

Note that we need to use
\mu\Rightarrow\mu\circ\mu\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}
and

Lemma

C.12
.3 to duplicate the lock

\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\mu_{F}}}}
in
(2)
and
(3)
for the handler
H
.
Then by
T-Abs
and
T-Mod
we have

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\;(\lambda y^{B^{\prime}}.{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\mu}\;\mathcal{E}[y]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H):{{{\mu}}}(B^{\prime}\to A)\;{{@}}\,{{F}}}\,(7)}

By
(4)
,
(5)
,
(7)
, and
Lemma

C.15
.3 we have

\Gamma\vdash N^{\prime}[U/p,({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\,(\lambda y^{B^{\prime}}.{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{\mu}\;\mathcal{E}[y]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H))/r]:B\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{F}}

∎

Appendix D
Source Calculi and Encodings

In this section, we provide the typing rules and operational semantics
of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
and
System C
that are omitted in
Sections

4.1
and
5.1
.
We also provide the translations of runtime constructs used in their
operational semantics.
Furthermore, we provide the specification of

System
\Xi

(
Brachthäuser et al. 2020
)
and its encoding in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
),
and the specification of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}

(
Xie et al. 2022
)
and its encoding in

Met
(
{\mathcal{R}_{\textsf{scp}}}
).

D.1.
Typing Rules and Operational Semantics of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}

Figure

11
gives the full typing rules of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
.

Figure

12
gives the operational semantics of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
including the definitions of runtime constructs and evaluation
contexts.
As we have mentioned in
Section

4.1
, they are pretty standard.

Typing rules for runtime constructs are as follows.

\frac{\Sigma\ni\ell:A^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime}\quad\quad\Gamma\vdash M:A\@ifmtarg{\ell,E}{}{\mid{\ell,E}}\quad\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:B^{\prime}\to^{E}A\vdash N:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N\}:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handle}}}

Translations of runtime constructs are as follows.
They are used in the proof of semantics preservation in

Section

E.1
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Computation}\to\text{Term}\\
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H:A\mid E\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}\;\llbracket M\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\llbracket H\rrbracket\end{array}

Translations of evaluation contexts are analogous to the translations
of their corresponding terms.

[formula]

\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash{()}:{\texttt{1}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Unit}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\ni x:A}{\Gamma\vdash x:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Var}}}\par\frac{\Gamma,x:A\vdash M:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}{\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{E}x^{A}.M:A\to^{E}B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Abs}}}\par\frac{\Gamma,\alpha:K\vdash V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}{\Gamma\vdash\Lambda\alpha^{K}.V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-TAbs}}}\par\frac{\Sigma\ni\ell:A^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:B^{\prime}\to^{E}A\vdash N:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{handler}}}\;\{\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N\}:({\texttt{1}}\to^{\ell,E}A)\to^{E}A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handler}}}

[formula]

\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Value}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,x:A\vdash N:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=M\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Let}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:A\to^{E}B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash W:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}{\Gamma\vdash V\;W:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-App}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:\forall\alpha^{K}.B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash A:K}{\Gamma\vdash V\;A:B[A/\alpha]\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-TApp}}}\par\frac{\Sigma\ni\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;V:B\@ifmtarg{\ell,E}{}{\mid{\ell,E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Do}}}

Figure 11.
Typing rules of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
.

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\quad}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\textrm{Computations}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&M&::=&\cdots\mid{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\\
\textrm{Evaluation Contexts}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\mathcal{E}&::=&[~]\mid{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=\mathcal{E}\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\mid{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;\mathcal{E}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\quad}@{}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\text{{E-TApp}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&(\Lambda\alpha^{K}.V)\,T&\mathrel{\leadsto}&V[T/\alpha]\\
\text{{E-App}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&(\lambda x^{A}.M)\,V&\mathrel{\leadsto}&M[V/x]\\
\text{{E-Handler}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{handler}}}\;H\;V&\mathrel{\leadsto}&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;V\;{()}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H,\\
\text{{E-Ret}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;({{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V)\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H&\mathrel{\leadsto}&{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\\
\text{{E-Op}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;V]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H&\mathrel{\leadsto}&N[V/p,(\lambda y.{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;y]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H)/r],\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\text{ where }\ell\notin\mathsf{bl}(\mathcal{E})\text{ and }H\ni(\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N)\\
\text{{E-Lift}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\mathcal{E}[M]&\mathrel{\leadsto}&\mathcal{E}[N],\hfill\text{if }M\mathrel{\leadsto}N\\
\end{array}

D.2.
Operational Semantics of
System C

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\quad}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\textrm{Runtime Labels}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\ell\\
\textrm{Runtime Contexts}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\Omega&::=&\cdot\mid\Omega,\ell:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B\\
\textrm{Capability Sets}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&C&::=&\cdot\mid\{\ell\}\mid\{f\}\mid C\cup C^{\prime}\\
\textrm{Blocks}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&P,Q&::=&\cdots\mid{{\mathbsf{cap}}}_{\ell}\\
\textrm{Computations}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&M&::=&\cdots\mid{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\\
\textrm{Evaluation Contexts}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\mathcal{E}&::=&[~]\mid{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=\mathcal{E}\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\mid{{\mathbsf{def}}}\;f=\mathcal{E}\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\mid{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;\mathcal{E}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\quad}@{}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\text{{E-Box}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{unbox}}}\;({{\mathbsf{box}}}\;P)&\mathrel{\leadsto}&P\\
\text{{E-Let}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x={{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N&\mathrel{\leadsto}&N[V/x]\\
\text{{E-Def}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{def}}}\;f=P\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N&\mathrel{\leadsto}&N[P/f]\\
\text{{E-Call}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\{(\@ifmtarg{\overline{x:A}}{\overline{f:T}}{\overline{x:A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{f:T}}{}{,\overline{f:T}}})\Rightarrow M\}(\overline{V},\overline{Q})&\mathrel{\leadsto}&M[\overline{V/x},\overline{Q/f},\overline{C/f}]\hskip 9.24994pt\text{where }\overline{\cdot\vdash Q:T\mid C}\\[3.87498pt]
\text{{E-Gen}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{try}}}\;\{f^{\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B}\Rightarrow M\}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\mid\Omega&\mathrel{\leadsto}&{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;M[{{\mathbsf{cap}}}_{\ell}/f,\{\ell\}/f]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\mid\Omega,\ell:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\text{where }\ell\text{ fresh}\\
\text{{E-Ret}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;({{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V)\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H&\mathrel{\leadsto}&{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\\
\text{{E-Op}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{cap}}}_{\ell}(V)]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H&\mathrel{\leadsto}&N[V/p,\{(\@ifmtarg{y}{}{y\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}})\Rightarrow{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;y]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\}/r],\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\text{ where }\ell\notin\mathsf{bl}(\mathcal{E})\text{ and }H=\{p\;r\mapsto N\}\\
\text{{E-Lift}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\mathcal{E}[M]&\mathrel{\leadsto}&\mathcal{E}[N],\hfill\text{if }M\mathrel{\leadsto}N\\
\end{array}

Figure 13.
Operational semantics and runtime constructs for
System C
.

Figure

13
defines the operational semantics and
syntax of runtime constructs for
System C
.
Reduction in
System C
is defined not only on terms but also blocks
since we have
{{\mathbsf{unbox}}}\;V
which can reduce.
Since
System C
uses block variables
f
as both term-level and
type-level variables, we need to substitute them separately.
We follow
Brachthäuser et al. 2022
to overload the notion of
substitution.
We write
C/f
for substituting in types and
P/f
for substituting in
terms.

Typing rules for runtime constructs are as follows.

\small\frac{\Omega\ni\ell:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B}{\Omega\mid\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{cap}}}_{\ell}:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B\@ifmtarg{\{\ell\}}{}{\mid{\{\ell\}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Cap}}}\par\frac{\Omega\ni\ell:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A^{\prime}}{}{A^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash M:A\@ifmtarg{C\cup\{\ell\}}{}{\mid{C\cup\{\ell\}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:^{C}(\@ifmtarg{B^{\prime}}{}{B^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}})\Rightarrow A\vdash N:A\@ifmtarg{C}{}{\mid{C}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Omega\mid\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{p\;r\mapsto N\}:A\@ifmtarg{C}{}{\mid{C}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handle}}}

Translations of runtime syntax are as follows.
They are used for semantics preservation.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Runtime Context}\to\text{Runtime Context}\\
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket&=&\cdot\\
\llbracket\Omega,\ell:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\Omega\rrbracket,\ell:\llbracket A\rrbracket\twoheadrightarrow\llbracket B\rrbracket\\[8.61108pt]
\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Term}\to\text{Term}\\[2.15277pt]
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{cap}}}_{\ell}\rrbracket&=&{\color[rgb]{0.543,0.5547,0.5625}{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle\rangle}}}\;(\lambda x^{\llbracket A\rrbracket}.{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;x)\quad\text{where }\Omega\ni\ell:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B\\[2.15277pt]
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H{:A\mid C}\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}\;\llbracket M\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\llbracket H^{{\ell,C}}\rrbracket\\[2.15277pt]
\llbracket\{p\;r\mapsto N\}^{{\ell,C}}\rrbracket&=&\{\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto{{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.543,0.5547,0.5625}{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\ell,\llbracket C\rrbracket]}}\;x^{\prime}=x\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;}x^{\prime},\\[2.15277pt]
\ell\;p\;r\mapsto{{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.543,0.5547,0.5625}{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}}\;\hat{r}=r\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;}\llbracket N\rrbracket\}\end{array}\end{array}

Translations of evaluation contexts are analogous to the translations
of their corresponding terms.

D.3.
System
\Xi
and its Encoding in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
)

System
\Xi

(
Brachthäuser et al. 2020
)
is a fragment of
System C
without
boxing and unboxing.
As a result, capabilities never appear in types.

System
\Xi
actually does not even track capabilities in the typing
judgements.
There is no danger of capability leakage since capabilities are
second-class.

Figure

14
gives the syntax and typing rules of

System
\Xi
.

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\quad}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\textrm{Value Types}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&A,B&::=&{\texttt{1}}\\
\textrm{Block Types}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&T&::=&(\@ifmtarg{\overline{A}}{\overline{T}}{\overline{A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{T}}{}{,\overline{T}}})\Rightarrow B\\
\textrm{Contexts}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\Gamma&::=&\cdot\mid\Gamma,x:A\mid\Gamma,f:T\\
\textrm{Values}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&V,W&::=&x\mid{()}\\
\textrm{Blocks}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&P,Q&::=&f\mid\{(\@ifmtarg{\overline{x:A}}{\overline{f:T}}{\overline{x:A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{f:T}}{}{,\overline{f:T}}})\Rightarrow M\}\\
\end{array}\hfill\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\quad}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\textrm{Computations}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&M,N&::=&{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\mid{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=M\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\\
\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&&\mid&{{\mathbsf{def}}}\;f=P\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\mid P(\overline{V},\overline{Q})\\
\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&&\mid&{{\mathbsf{try}}}\;\{f^{\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B}\Rightarrow M\}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\\
\textrm{Handlers}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&H&::=&\{p\;r\mapsto N\}\\
\end{array}

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash{()}:{\texttt{1}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Unit}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\ni x:A}{\Gamma\vdash x:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Var}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\ni f:T}{\Gamma\vdash f:T\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-BlockVar}}}\par\frac{\Gamma,\overline{x:A},\overline{f:T}\vdash M:B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}{\Gamma\vdash\{(\@ifmtarg{\overline{x:A}}{\overline{f:T}}{\overline{x:A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{f:T}}{}{,\overline{f:T}}})\Rightarrow M\}:(\@ifmtarg{\overline{A}}{\overline{T}}{\overline{A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{T}}{}{,\overline{T}}})\Rightarrow B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Block}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Value}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,x:A\vdash N:B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=M\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N:B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Let}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash P:T\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,f:T\vdash N:B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{def}}}\;f=P\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N:B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Def}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash P:(\@ifmtarg{\overline{A}_{i}}{\overline{T}_{j}}{\overline{A}_{i}\@ifmtarg{\overline{T}_{j}}{}{,\overline{T}_{j}}})\Rightarrow B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\overline{\Gamma\vdash V_{i}:A_{i}\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\overline{\Gamma\vdash Q_{j}:T_{j}\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}}{\Gamma\vdash P(\overline{V}_{i},\overline{Q}_{j}):B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Call}}}\par\frac{\Gamma,f:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A^{\prime}}{}{A^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}\vdash M:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{B^{\prime}}{}{B^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow A\vdash N:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{try}}}\;\{f^{\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A^{\prime}}{}{A^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}}\Rightarrow M\}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{p\;r\mapsto N\}:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Handle}}}

Figure 14.
Syntax and typing rules for
System
\Xi
.

The operational semantics of
System
\Xi
is almost identical to that of

System C
except for removing the
E-Box
rule and substitutions
of capability sets
C/f
.

Figure

15
gives the encoding of
System
\Xi
into

Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
This encoding is straightforward and does not even use any modalities.
We mostly just translate the syntax of second-class blocks in

System
\Xi
to first-class functions in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
For named handlers we introduce local labels.
We use the syntactic sugar in
Section

3.5
for
{{\mathbsf{handle}}}

with no modality annotation.

\begin{array}[t]{r@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Type}\to\text{Type}\\
\llbracket{\texttt{1}}\rrbracket&=&{\texttt{1}}\\
\llbracket(\@ifmtarg{\overline{A}}{\overline{T}}{\overline{A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{T}}{}{,\overline{T}}})\Rightarrow B\rrbracket&=&\overline{\llbracket A\rrbracket}\to\overline{\llbracket T\rrbracket}\to\llbracket B\rrbracket\\[7.74997pt]
\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Context}\to\text{Context}\\
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket&=&\cdot\\
\llbracket\Gamma,x:A\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,x:\llbracket A\rrbracket\\
\llbracket\Gamma,f:T\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,f:\llbracket T\rrbracket\\[7.74997pt]
\end{array}\begin{array}[t]{r@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Value}\to\text{Term}\\
\llbracket x\rrbracket&=&x\\
\llbracket{()}\rrbracket&=&{()}\\[7.74997pt]
\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Block}\to\text{Term}\\
\llbracket f\rrbracket&=&f\\
\llbracket\{(\@ifmtarg{\overline{x:A}}{\overline{f:T}}{\overline{x:A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{f:T}}{}{,\overline{f:T}}})\Rightarrow M\}\rrbracket&=&\lambda\overline{x^{\llbracket A\rrbracket}}\,\overline{f^{\llbracket T\rrbracket}}.\llbracket M\rrbracket\\[7.74997pt]
\end{array}

\begin{array}[t]{r@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Computation}\to\text{Term}\\
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\rrbracket&=&\llbracket V\rrbracket\\
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=M\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=\llbracket M\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;\llbracket N\rrbracket\\
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{def}}}\;f=P\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;f=\llbracket P\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;\llbracket N\rrbracket\\
\llbracket P(\overline{V},\overline{Q})\rrbracket&=&\llbracket P\rrbracket\;\overline{\llbracket V\rrbracket}\;\overline{\llbracket Q\rrbracket}\\
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{try}}}\;\{f^{\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B}\Rightarrow M\}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\rrbracket&=&\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{\mathbsf{local}}}\;\ell_{f}:\llbracket A\rrbracket\to\llbracket B\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\\
{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;(\lambda f.\llbracket M\rrbracket)\;(\lambda x^{\llbracket A\rrbracket}.{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell_{f}\;x)\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\llbracket H^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{f}}}\rrbracket\end{array}\\
\llbracket\{p\;r\mapsto N\}^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{f}}}\rrbracket&=&\{{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle\ell_{f}\rangle}}\;x^{\prime}=x\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;x^{\prime},\ell_{f}\;p\;r\mapsto\llbracket N\rrbracket\}\\
\end{array}

Figure 15.
An encoding of
System
\Xi
in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).

We translate runtime constructs used in the operational semantics as follows.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket{{\mathbsf{cap}}}_{\ell}\rrbracket&=&\lambda x^{\llbracket A\rrbracket}.{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell\;x\quad\text{where }\Omega\ni\ell:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A}{}{A\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B\\[2.15277pt]
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;\llbracket M\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\llbracket H^{\ell}\rrbracket\\[2.15277pt]
\llbracket\{p\;r\mapsto N\}^{\ell}\rrbracket&=&\{{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle\ell\rangle}}\;x^{\prime}=x\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;x^{\prime},\ell\;p\;r\mapsto\llbracket N\rrbracket\}\end{array}

We have the following theorems which we prove in
Section

E.3
.

Theorem D.1 (Type Preservation).

If
\,\Gamma\vdash M:A
in
System
\Xi
, then
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\cdot}}
in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
Similarly for values and blocks.

Theorem D.2 (Semantics Preservation).

If
M
is well-typed and
M\mid\Omega\mathrel{\leadsto}N\mid{\Omega^{\prime}}
in

System
\Xi
, then
\llbracket M\rrbracket\mid{\llbracket\Omega\rrbracket}\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\ast}\llbracket N\rrbracket\mid{\llbracket\Omega^{\prime}\rrbracket}

in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).

D.4.
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
and its Encoding in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
)

Figure

16
gives the syntax of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
. Our presentation
of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
is fine-grain call-by-value. We highlight new parts
compared to
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
.
Each effect label
\ell
is annotated with a scope variable
a
.
As before, we omit kinds when obvious from alphabets.

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\quad}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\textrm{Value Types}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&A,B&::=&{\texttt{1}}\mid\alpha\mid A\to^{E}B\mid\forall\alpha^{K}.A\mid\mathchoice{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\displaystyle\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\textstyle\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}$}}}\\
\textrm{Kind}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&K&::=&\mathsf{Value}\mid\mathsf{Effect}\mid\mathchoice{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\displaystyle\mathsf{Scope}(\ell)$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\textstyle\mathsf{Scope}(\ell)$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Scope}(\ell)$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Scope}(\ell)$}}}\\
\textrm{Effect Rows}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&E&::=&\cdot\mid\varepsilon\mid\mathchoice{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\displaystyle\ell^{a}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\textstyle\ell^{a}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptstyle\ell^{a}$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptscriptstyle\ell^{a}$}}},E\\
\textrm{Contexts}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\Gamma&::=&\cdot\mid\Gamma,x:A\mid\Gamma,\alpha:K\\
\textrm{Values}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&V,W&::=&{()}\mid x\mid\lambda^{E}x^{A}.M\mid\Lambda\alpha^{K}.V\mid\mathchoice{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\displaystyle{{\mathbsf{nhandler}}}\;H$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\textstyle{{\mathbsf{nhandler}}}\;H$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptstyle{{\mathbsf{nhandler}}}\;H$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbsf{nhandler}}}\;H$}}}\\
\textrm{Computations}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&M,N&::=&{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\mid V\;W\mid V\;A\mid{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=M\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\mid\mathchoice{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\displaystyle{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;V\;W$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\textstyle{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;V\;W$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptstyle{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;V\;W$}}}{{\lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.4}\hbox{\pagecolor{hlcolor}\lxSVG@fill@opacity{1}$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;V\;W$}}}\\
\textrm{Handlers}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&H&::=&\{\ell\;p\;r\mapsto M\}\\
\textrm{Label Contexts}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\Sigma&::=&\cdot\mid\Sigma,\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\\
\end{array}

Figure 16.
Syntax of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
.

Different from
Xie et al. 2022
, the kind
\mathsf{Scope}(\ell)
of scope
variables is annotated with an effect label
\ell
.
For
a:\mathsf{Scope}(\ell)
, each appearance of
a
in effect rows must be
associated with this label
\ell
as
\ell^{a}
.
This annotation is important to rule out well-typed but meaningless
terms in
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
.
Not every well-typed term in
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
is meaningful in the sense that we
can find an appropriate evaluation context to fully apply its
abstractions and handle its effects.
For example, a function of type
\forall a.\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell_{1}}{a}{{\ell_{1}}^{a}}\times\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell_{2}}{a}{{\ell_{2}}^{a}}\to^{\ell_{1}^{a},\ell_{2}^{a}}{\texttt{1}}
cannot be applied and
handled when
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
, because named handlers cannot
provide two evidences values of types
\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell_{1}}{a}{{\ell_{1}}^{a}}
and

\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell_{2}}{a}{{\ell_{2}}^{a}}
with the same scope variable
a
but different
operations
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
. (This type becomes meaningful with
umbrella effects in
Xie et al. 2022
.)
Each handler introduces its own scope variable
a
with some fixed
operation label
\ell
.
Annotating the kind
\mathsf{Scope}
with an operation label
\ell
solves the
problem of attaching the same scope variable
a
to different
operation labels.

Figure

17
gives the typing rules.
Rule
T-NamedHandler
introduces a named handler as a function,
whose argument takes a handler name of the evidence type

\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}
.
An evidence type specifies an effect
\ell
and a scope variable
a
.
The usage of rank-2 polymorphism guarantees that the handler name of
type
\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}
cannot escape the scope of the handler.
Rule
T-DoName
invokes an operation via a handler name
M
of the
evidence type
\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}
.

[formula]

\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash{()}:{\texttt{1}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Unit}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\ni x:A}{\Gamma\vdash x:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Var}}}\par\frac{\Gamma,x:A\vdash M:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}{\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{E}x^{A}.M:A\to^{E}B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Abs}}}\par\frac{\Gamma,\alpha:K\vdash V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}{\Gamma\vdash\Lambda\alpha^{K}.V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-TAbs}}}\par\frac{\Sigma\ni\ell:A^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:B^{\prime}\to^{E}A\vdash N:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Gamma\vdash\text{${{\mathbsf{nhandler}}}$}\;\{\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N\}:(\forall a^{\mathsf{Scope}(\ell)}.\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}\to^{\ell^{a},E}A)\to^{E}A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-NamedHandler}}}

[formula]

\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Value}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:A\to^{E}B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash W:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}{\Gamma\vdash V\;W:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-App}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash V:\forall\alpha^{K}.B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash A:K}{\Gamma\vdash V\;A:B[A/\alpha]\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-TApp}}}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma,x:A\vdash N:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=M\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Let}}}\par\frac{\Sigma\ni\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash V:\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Gamma\vdash W:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}\hskip 9.24994pt}{\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;V\;W:B\@ifmtarg{\ell^{a},E}{}{\mid{\ell^{a},E}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-DoName}}}

Figure 17.
Typing rules for
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
.

Figure

18
gives the operational semantics of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
including definitions of runtime constructs and evaluation contexts.
Similar to the operational semantics of
Met
(
\mathcal{X}
) and
System C
, reduction
rules in
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
are also of form
M\mid\Omega\mathrel{\leadsto}N\mid\Omega^{\prime}
.
The most interesting rule is
E-Gen
which reduces a handler
application to a runtime
{{\mathbsf{handle}}}
construct and passes a runtime
evidence value
{{\mathbsf{ev}}}_{h}
to the argument.
This rule generates a fresh marker
h
and a fresh scope variable
a
.
The runtime context
\Omega
associates dynamically generated
markers
h
to their operation label
\ell
and dynamically generated
scope variable
a
.

Different from
Xie et al. 2022
, our
E-Op
rule for
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
has
the condition
h\notin\mathsf{BH}(\mathcal{E})
which makes sure there is no
other handler in
\mathcal{E}
with the marker
h
.
This condition is necessary to guarantee that the current handler is
the nearest one for the marker
h
, because even for named handlers it
is still possible to duplicate the same handler during the runtime as
observed by
Biernacki et al. 2020
.

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\quad}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\textrm{Runtime Markers}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&h\\
\textrm{Runtime Contexts}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\Omega&::=&\cdot\mid\Omega,h:\ell^{a}\\
\textrm{Computations}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&M&::=&\cdots\mid{{\mathbsf{handle}}}_{h}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\\
\textrm{Values}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&V&::=&\cdots\mid{{\mathbsf{ev}}}_{h}\\
\textrm{Evaluation Contexts}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\mathcal{E}&::=&[~]\mid{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=\mathcal{E}\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\mid{{\mathbsf{handle}}}_{h}\;\mathcal{E}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\quad}@{}r@{~}c@{~}l@{}}\text{{E-TApp}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&(\Lambda\alpha^{K}.V)\,A&\mathrel{\leadsto}&V[A/\alpha]\\
\text{{E-App}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&(\lambda x^{A}.M)\,V&\mathrel{\leadsto}&M[V/x]\\
\text{{E-Gen}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{nhandler}}}\;H\;V\mid\Omega&\mathrel{\leadsto}&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}_{h}\;({{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=V\,a\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;x\;{{\mathbsf{ev}}}_{h})\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\mid\Omega,h:\ell^{a}\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\text{where }a,h\text{ fresh and }H\ni(\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N)\\
\text{{E-NRet}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}_{h}\;({{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V)\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H&\mathrel{\leadsto}&{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\\
\text{{E-NOp}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}_{h}\;\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;{{\mathbsf{ev}}}_{h}\;V]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H&\mathrel{\leadsto}&N[V/p,(\lambda y.{{\mathbsf{handle}}}_{h}\;\mathcal{E}[{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;y]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H)/r],\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\text{ where }\ell\notin\mathsf{BH}(\mathcal{E})\text{ and }H\ni(\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N)\\
\text{{E-Lift}}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\mathcal{E}[M]&\mathrel{\leadsto}&\mathcal{E}[N],\hfill\text{if }M\mathrel{\leadsto}N\\
\end{array}

Figure 18.
Operational semantics for
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
.

Typing rules for runtime constructs are as follows.

\frac{\Omega\ni h:\ell^{a}}{\Omega\mid\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{ev}}}_{h}:\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Evidence}}}\par\frac{\Omega\ni h:\ell^{a}\quad\Sigma\ni\ell:A^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime}\quad\quad\Gamma\vdash M:A\@ifmtarg{\ell^{a},E}{}{\mid{\ell^{a},E}}\quad\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:B^{\prime}\to^{E}A\vdash N:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}\quad}{\Omega\mid\Gamma\vdash{{\mathbsf{handle}}}_{h}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{\ell\;p\;r\mapsto N\}:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-HandleName}}}

Figure

19
gives the translation of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
into
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
The evidence type
\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}
with
\Sigma\ni\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B
is
translated to a function type
{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[a]}}}(\llbracket A\rrbracket\to\llbracket B\rrbracket)
.
Correspondingly, an operation invocation
{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;V\;W
for

V:\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}
is translated similarly to a function application.
A named handler
{{\mathbsf{nhandler}}}\;H
is translated to a function that takes
a function argument
f
and handles it with a handler in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
We pass the term
{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[a]}\;(\lambda x.{{\mathbsf{do}}}_{a}\;x)
to the
argument
f
to simulate the handler name of type
\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}
in

System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
.

\begin{array}[t]{r@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Kind}\to\text{Kind}\\
\llbracket\mathsf{Value}\rrbracket&=&\mathsf{Abs}\\
\llbracket\mathsf{Effect}\rrbracket&=&\mathsf{Effect}\\
\llbracket\mathsf{Scope}(\ell)\rrbracket&=&\mathsf{Effect}\\[7.74997pt]
\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Type}\to\text{Type}\\
\llbracket{\texttt{1}}\rrbracket&=&{\texttt{1}}\\
\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket&=&\alpha\\
\llbracket A\to^{E}B\rrbracket&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}}}(\llbracket A\rrbracket\to\llbracket B\rrbracket)\\
\llbracket\forall\alpha^{K}.A\rrbracket&=&\forall\alpha^{\llbracket K\rrbracket}.\llbracket A\rrbracket\\
\llbracket\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}\rrbracket&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[a]}}}(\llbracket A\rrbracket\to\llbracket B\rrbracket)\\
&&\text{where }\Sigma\ni\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\\
\end{array}\begin{array}[t]{r@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Effect Row}\to\text{Effect Context}\\
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket&=&\cdot\\
\llbracket\varepsilon\rrbracket&=&\varepsilon\\
\llbracket\ell^{a},E\rrbracket&=&a,\llbracket E\rrbracket\\[7.74997pt]
\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Context}\to\text{Context}\\
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket&=&\cdot\\
\llbracket\Gamma,x:A\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,x:\llbracket A\rrbracket\\
\llbracket\Gamma,\alpha:K\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\alpha:\llbracket K\rrbracket\\[7.74997pt]
\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Label Context}\to\text{Label Context}\\
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket&=&\cdot\\
\llbracket\Sigma,\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\Sigma\rrbracket,\ell:\llbracket A\rrbracket\twoheadrightarrow\llbracket B\rrbracket\end{array}

\begin{array}[t]{r@{\ \ }c@{\ \ }l}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Value / Computation}\to\text{Term}\\
\llbracket{()}\rrbracket&=&{()}\\
\llbracket x\rrbracket&=&x\\
\llbracket\Lambda\alpha^{K}.V\rrbracket&=&\Lambda\alpha^{\llbracket K\rrbracket}.\llbracket V\rrbracket\\
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\rrbracket&=&\llbracket V\rrbracket\\
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=M\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=\llbracket M\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;\llbracket N\rrbracket\\
\llbracket V\;A\rrbracket&=&\llbracket V\rrbracket\;\llbracket A\rrbracket\\
\llbracket V^{A\to^{E}B}\;W\rrbracket&=&\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}\;x=\llbracket V\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;x\;\llbracket W\rrbracket\\
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;V^{\mathsf{ev}\,\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}}\;W\rrbracket&=&\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[a]}\;x=\llbracket V\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;x\;\llbracket W\rrbracket\\
\Bigl\llbracket{\vbox{\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{\mathbsf{nhandler}}}\; H\\
:(\forall a^\ell.
\mathsf{ev}\,
\@ifmtarg{\ell}{a}{{\ell}^{a}}
\to^{\ell^a,E} A) \to^{E} A\end{array}}}\Bigr\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}\;(\lambda f.\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{\mathbsf{local}}}\;\ell_{a}:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\twoheadrightarrow\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\\
{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}\;(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\ell_{a},\llbracket E\rrbracket]}\;f^{\prime}=f\;\ell_{a}\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\\
f^{\prime}\;({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\ell_{a}]}\;(\lambda x^{\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket}.{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell_{a}\;x)))\end{array}\\
\!\!\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\llbracket H^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{a,E}}}\rrbracket)\end{array}\\
\llbracket H^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{a,E}}}\rrbracket&=&\{\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{\mathbsf{return}}}\;x\mapsto\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\ell_{a},\llbracket E\rrbracket]}\;x=x\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;x,\\
\ell\;p\;r\mapsto\llbracket N\rrbracket\}\end{array}\\
\end{array}

Figure 19.
An encoding of
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
in

(

).

The following theorems show that our encoding preserves types and
operational semantics.

Theorem D.3 (Type Preservation).

If
\,\Gamma\vdash M:A\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}
in
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
, then
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\llbracket E\rrbracket}}
in

Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
Similarly for typing judgements of values.

Lemma D.4 (Semantics Preservation).

If
M
is well-typed and
M\mathrel{\leadsto}N
in
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
, then
\llbracket M\rrbracket\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\ast}\llbracket N\rrbracket
in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
) where

\mathrel{\leadsto}^{\ast}
denotes the transitive closure of
\mathrel{\leadsto}
.

Translations of runtime constructs for
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
are as follows.
Note that for dynamically generated scope variables
a
, we translate
to their corresponding dynamically generated labels
\ell_{a}
where

\Omega\ni h:\ell^{a}
.
The translations of terms do not use
h
as the scope variable
a
is
also dynamically generated and is enough to uniquely assign operations
to handlers.
The translations are used in the proof of semantics preservation in

Section

E.4
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Runtime Context}\to\text{Runtime Context}\\
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket&=&\cdot\\
\llbracket\Omega,h:\ell^{a}\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\Omega\rrbracket,\ell_{a}:\llbracket A\rrbracket\twoheadrightarrow\llbracket B\rrbracket\hfill\text{ where }\Sigma\ni\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\\[8.61108pt]
\llbracket-\rrbracket&:&\text{Term}\to\text{Term}\\[2.15277pt]
\llbracket V\;a\rrbracket&=&\llbracket V\rrbracket\;\ell_{a}\quad\text{where }\Omega\ni h:\ell^{a}\\[2.15277pt]
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{ev}}}_{h}\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\ell_{a}]}\;(\lambda x^{\llbracket A\rrbracket}.{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell_{a}\;x)\\
&&\quad\text{where }\Omega\ni h:\ell^{a}\text{ and }\Sigma\ni\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\\[2.15277pt]
\llbracket{{\mathbsf{handle}}}_{h}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{:A\mid E}}\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}\;\llbracket M\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\llbracket H\rrbracket\\
&&\quad\text{where }\Omega\ni h:\ell^{a}\text{ and }\Sigma\ni\ell:A\twoheadrightarrow B\\
\end{array}

Translations of evaluation contexts are analogous to the translations
of their corresponding terms.

Appendix E
Proofs of Encodings

We prove the type preservation and semantics preservation theorems in

Sections

5.2
and
4.2
.
We also prove the type preservation and semantics preservation
theorems for some other encodings.

E.1.
Proofs of Encoding of

\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
in

(
{\mathcal{R}_{\textsf{scp}}}
)

See
4.1

Proof.

By induction on typing judgements
\Gamma\vdash M:A\mid E
in

System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
.
Most cases follow from using IH trivially. We elaborate interesting cases.
When referring to the name of a rule, we sometimes also mention the
calculus name to disambiguate. For instance,

-

\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}

refers to the rule

of

\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}

) and thus can always be accessed by

-

(
{\mathcal{R}_{\textsf{scp}}}
)
.

and

-

(

)

and

-

(
{\mathcal{R}_{\textsf{scp}}}
)
.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0.2,0.2}{\Gamma,x:A\vdash M:B\@ifmtarg{E}{}{\mid{E}}}\,(1)}}{\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{E}x^{A}.M:A\to^{E}B\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}}\quad\textsc{Lab=\text{{T-Abs}}}

\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}}},x:\llbracket A\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket:\llbracket B\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\llbracket E\rrbracket}}

The proof relies on the following lemma.

Lemma E.1 (Pure Values).

Given a typing judgement
\Gamma\vdash V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}
in
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
,
if
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket V\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\cdot}}

then
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket V\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\llbracket E\rrbracket}}
for any
E
.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on typing judgements of values in

System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
.
∎

See
4.2

Proof.

M
and case analysis on the next reduction rule.

System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
are translated to value normal forms in
Met
(
{\mathcal{R}_{\textsf{scp}}}
).

.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{{\mathbsf{handler}}}\;H\;V&\mathrel{\leadsto}&{{\mathbsf{handle}}}\;V\;{()}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\end{array}

\llbracket{{\mathbsf{handler}}}\;H\rrbracket&=&{{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}}\;(\lambda f.{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{[\llbracket E\rrbracket]}\;({{\mathbsf{let}}}\;{{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket\ell,E\rrbracket]}}\;f^{\prime}=f\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;f^{\prime}\;{()})\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\llbracket H^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{E}}}\rrbracket)\end{array}

and

in

(
{\mathcal{R}_{\textsf{scp}}}

which is equal to

of the above reduction step.

\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
.

As a visual aid, for each non-trivial case we repeat its typing rule
in

translated conclusion that we need to prove.
When referring to the name of a rule, we sometimes also mention the
calculus name to disambiguate. For instance,

System C

refers to the rule

of

\llbracket(\@ifmtarg{\overline{A_{i}}}{\overline{f_{j}:T_{j}}}{\overline{A_{i}}\@ifmtarg{\overline{f_{j}:T_{j}}}{}{,\overline{f_{j}:T_{j}}}})\Rightarrow B\rrbracket=\forall\overline{{f}^{\ast}_{j}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle\overline{{f}^{\ast}_{j}}\rangle}}}}(\overline{\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}\to\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[{f}^{\ast}_{j}]}}}\llbracket T_{j}\rrbracket}\to\llbracket B\rrbracket)
.
By
(1)
and
Lemma

E.3
we have

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\overline{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket V_{i}\rrbracket:\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket\;{{@}}\,{{C^{\prime}}}}}\,(3)}\\
\end{array}

By
(2)
and
Lemma

C.14
, we have

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{\overline{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[\llbracket C_{j}\rrbracket]}}}\vdash\llbracket Q_{j}\rrbracket:\llbracket T_{j}\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\llbracket C_{j}\rrbracket}}}}\end{array}

Then by
T-

-
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
)
we have

Also note that translation preserves type substitution of capability variables, which gives

{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{\llbracket B\rrbracket[\overline{\llbracket C_{j}\rrbracket/{f}^{\ast}_{j}}]=\llbracket B[\overline{C_{j}/f_{j}}]\rrbracket}\,(5)}.

Finally our goal follows from

,

\boxed{{{\mathbsf{try}}}\;\{f^{\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A^{\prime}}{}{A^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}}\Rightarrow M\}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\{p\;r\mapsto N\}}

\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0.2,0.2}{\llbracket\Gamma,{f:^{\ast}(\@ifmtarg{A^{\prime}}{}{A^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}})\Rightarrow B^{\prime}}\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{{@}}\,{{\llbracket C\cup\{f\}\rrbracket}}}\,(1)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,1}{\llbracket\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:^{C}(\@ifmtarg{B^{\prime}}{}{B^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}})\Rightarrow A\rrbracket_{C}\vdash\llbracket N\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{{@}}\,{{\llbracket C\rrbracket}}}\,(2)}\quad}{\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{C}\vdash{\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{{\mathbsf{local}}}\;\ell_{f}:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\twoheadrightarrow\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;}\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle\ell_{f}\rangle}}\;g\;=M_{1}\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\\[2.15277pt]

where

\begin{array}[]{r@{~}c@{~}l}M_{1}&=&({\Lambda{f}^{\ast}}.{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle{f}^{\ast}\rangle}}\;(\lambda f^{{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[{f}^{\ast}]}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle\rangle}}}}(\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\to\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket)}.\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[{f}^{\ast}]}\;\hat{f}=f\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;\llbracket M\rrbracket))\;\ell_{f}\\[2.15277pt]
M_{2}&=&{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\ell_{f}]}\;({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle\rangle}}\;(\lambda x^{\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket}.{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell_{f}\;x))\\[2.15277pt]

N_{2}&=&{\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}\;\hat{r}=r\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;}\llbracket N\rrbracket\end{array}

For the translations of contexts in

and

, we have

\begin{array}[]{l@{~}c@{~}l}{\llbracket\Gamma,{f:^{\ast}(\@ifmtarg{A^{\prime}}{}{A^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}})\Rightarrow B^{\prime}}\rrbracket}&=&\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,{f}^{\ast},f:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[{f}^{\ast}]}}}(\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\to\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket),\hat{f}\@ifmtarg{[{f}^{\ast}]}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[{f}^{\ast}]}}}}\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\to\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\\
{\llbracket\Gamma,p:A^{\prime},r:^{C}(\@ifmtarg{B^{\prime}}{}{B^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}})\Rightarrow A\rrbracket}&=&\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,p:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket,r:{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}}}(\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket\to\llbracket A\rrbracket),\hat{r}\@ifmtarg{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}{:}{:_{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}}}}\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket\to\llbracket A\rrbracket\end{array}\end{array}
