The proof relies on the following lemma.

Lemma E.3 (Subeffecting).

Given a typing judgement
\Gamma\vdash M:A\mid C
in
System C
,
if
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\llbracket C\rrbracket}}

and
C\subseteq C^{\prime}
then
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket}}
. Similarly for blocks.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on typing judgements in
System C
.
∎

See
5.2

Proof.

By induction on
M
and case analysis on the next reduction rule.
Values in
System C
are translated to values in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
Not all values in
System C
are translated to value normal forms in

Met
(
\mathcal{S}
), but we can always further reduce them to value normal forms
in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
The theorems allows us to have more steps of reduction in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).
We do not explicitly mention that we reduce translations of values
to value normal forms in the following proof.

Case

\boxed{\text{{E-Box}}}

We have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket{{\mathbsf{unbox}}}\;({{\mathbsf{box}}}\;P)\rrbracket&=&\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}\;x={{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}\;\llbracket P\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;x\end{array}

By
E-
Letmod
-
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
)
.

Case

\boxed{\text{{E-Let}}}
We have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x={{\mathbsf{return}}}\;V\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;x=\llbracket V\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;\llbracket N\rrbracket\end{array}

LHS reduces to
N[V/x]
and RHS reduces to
\llbracket N\rrbracket[\llbracket V\rrbracket/x]
.
It is easy to show that translation preserves value substitution.

Case

\boxed{\text{{E-Def}}}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{{\mathbsf{def}}}\;f=P\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N&\mathrel{\leadsto}&N[P/f]\\
\end{array}

By translation preserving substitution, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\mathrm{LHS}\rrbracket&=&{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;f={{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}}\,\llbracket P\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;{{\mathbsf{let}}}\;{{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}\;}\hat{f}=f\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;\llbracket N\rrbracket\\[4.30554pt]
\llbracket\mathrm{RHS}\rrbracket&=&\llbracket N\rrbracket[\llbracket P\rrbracket/\hat{f}]\end{array}

Our goal follows from
E-
App
-
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
)
and
E-
Letmod
-
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
)
.

Case

\boxed{\text{{E-Call}}}

\{(\@ifmtarg{\overline{x:A}}{\overline{f:T}}{\overline{x:A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{f:T}}{}{,\overline{f:T}}})\Rightarrow M\}(\overline{V},\overline{Q})\mathrel{\leadsto}M[\overline{V/x},\overline{Q/f},\overline{C/f}]

Let
P={\{(\@ifmtarg{\overline{x:A}}{\overline{f:T}}{\overline{x:A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{f:T}}{}{,\overline{f:T}}})\Rightarrow M\}}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket P\rrbracket&=&\Lambda\overline{{f}^{\ast}}.{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle\overline{{f}^{\ast}}\rangle}}(\lambda\overline{x^{\llbracket A\rrbracket}}\,\overline{f^{{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[{f}^{\ast}]}}}\llbracket T\rrbracket}}.\overline{\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[{f}^{\ast}]}\;\hat{f}=f\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;}\llbracket M\rrbracket)\\[4.30554pt]
\llbracket P(\overline{V_{i}},\overline{Q_{j}})\rrbracket&=&\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle\overline{\llbracket C_{j}\rrbracket}\rangle}}\;x=\llbracket P\rrbracket\;\overline{\llbracket C_{j}\rrbracket}\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;x\;\overline{\llbracket V_{i}\rrbracket}\;\overline{({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\llbracket C_{j}\rrbracket]}\,\llbracket Q_{j}\rrbracket)}\end{array}

Our goal follows from
E-Letmod
,
E-App
and

E-TApp
in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
), as well as the fact that translation
preserves value substitution and type substitution.

Case

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{{\mathbsf{try}}}\;\{f^{\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A^{\prime}}{}{A^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}}\Rightarrow M\}\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\mid\Omega&\mathrel{\leadsto}&{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{\ell}\;M[{{\mathbsf{cap}}}_{\ell}/f,\{\ell\}/f]\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\mid\Omega,\ell:\@ifmtarg{}{}{(}\@ifmtarg{A^{\prime}}{}{A^{\prime}\@ifmtarg{}{}{,}}\@ifmtarg{}{}{)}\Rightarrow B^{\prime}\end{array}

\ell
fresh.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket{{\mathbsf{try}}}_{f}\;M\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;H\rrbracket&=&\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{{\mathbsf{local}}}\;\ell_{f}:\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\twoheadrightarrow\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;}{\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle\ell_{f}\rangle}}\;}g\;=\\[2.15277pt]
({\Lambda{f}^{\ast}}.{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{{\langle{f}^{\ast}\rangle}}\;(\lambda f^{{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{[{f}^{\ast}]}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{{\langle\rangle}}}}(\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\to\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket)}.{\@ifmtarg{}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}{{{\mathbsf{let}}}}\;{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[{f}^{\ast}]}\;\hat{f}=f\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;}\llbracket M\rrbracket){)\;\ell_{f}}\\[4.30554pt]
\!\!\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;{{\mathbsf{handle}}}^{[\llbracket C\rrbracket]}\;(g\;({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{[\ell_{f}]}\;({{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\langle\rangle}\;(\lambda x^{\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket}.{{\mathbsf{do}}}\;\ell_{f}\;x))))\;{{\mathbsf{with}}}\;\llbracket H\rrbracket\end{array}\end{array}

\ell
as in the reduction of

, the

The proof of semantics preservation relies on the following lemma.

Lemma E.4 (Translation of Evaluation Contexts).

For the translation
\llbracket-\rrbracket
from
System C
to
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
), we have

\llbracket\mathcal{E}[M]\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathcal{E}\rrbracket[\llbracket M\rrbracket]
for any
evaluation context
\mathcal{E}
and term
M
.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on evaluation contexts of
System C
.
For the case of
{{\mathbsf{def}}}\;f=\mathcal{E}\;{{\mathbsf{in}}}\;N
, note that
{{\mathbsf{mod}}}_{\mu}\;\llbracket\mathcal{E}\rrbracket
is a
valid evaluation context in
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
).

E.3.
Proofs of Encoding

Proof.

\Xi
.
We prove a stronger version which says that

\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\vdash\llbracket M\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{E}}

\mathcal{S}

We need this stronger version to prove the case of named handlers.
As a visual aid, for each non-trivial case we repeat its typing rule
in

\Xi
. We replace each premise by the
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
) judgement of
the translated premise implied by the induction hypothesis. We
replace the conclusion by the
Met
(
\mathcal{S}
) judgement of the
translated conclusion that we need to prove.
When referring to the name of a rule, we sometimes also mention the
calculus name to disambiguate. For instance,
T-
Var
-
System
\Xi

refers to the rule
T-Var
of
System
\Xi
.

{\llbracket(\@ifmtarg{\overline{A}}{\overline{T}}{\overline{A}\@ifmtarg{\overline{T}}{}{,\overline{T}}})\Rightarrow B\rrbracket}=\overline{\llbracket A\rrbracket}\to\overline{\llbracket T\rrbracket}\to\llbracket B\rrbracket

The proof of type preservation relies on the following lemma.

Lemma E.6 (Pure Values).

Given a typing judgement
\Gamma\vdash V:A\@ifmtarg{}{}{\mid{}}
in
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
,
if
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{\cdot}\vdash\llbracket V\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\cdot}}

then
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{E}\vdash\llbracket V\rrbracket:\llbracket A\rrbracket\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{@}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1797,0.4023,0.9727}{\llbracket E\rrbracket}}
for any
E
.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on typing judgements of values in

System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon+\mathsf{sn}}
.
The most non-trivial case is to show the accessibility of variables.
Observe that the change from
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{\cdot}
to

\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{E}
only changes the translations of locks.
After translation, all variables in the context have types of
kind
\mathsf{Abs}
.
Their accessibility follows from
Lemma

C.14
.
∎

See
D.4

Proof.

M
and case analysis on the next reduction rule.

System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
are translated to value normal forms in
Met
(
{\mathcal{R}_{\textsf{scp}}}
).
Most cases are similar to those in the proof of encoding
System
\textsf{F}^{\epsilon}
in

Section

E.1
.
We show new cases relevant to named handlers.
